Key judgement #2 – Restrictive

financial conditions will contribute to
the emergence of some spare
capacity in the economy and the
labour market, helping reduce
inflationary pressures.
The forecasts are conditioned on a market path for the
cash rate that increases by 35 basis points by mid-2027,
before declining a little to stabilise at 4.8 per cent in
2028. This is up to 50 basis points higher than the path
that underpinned the August forecasts. Conditioned on
that path, the forecast is for a period of below-potential

growth that is sufficient to generate some spare capacity
in the economy and help return inflation to target. In
other words, the assumed path for policy is judged to
be restrictive.
One way to cross-check the plausibility of this
judgement is to compare the market path with current
estimates of the neutral cash rate derived from models
and market economists. The assumed cash rate path lies
above the top of the range of these estimates (see
Chapter 1: Financial Conditions). These estimates are

subject to considerable uncertainty, however, and it is
common for them to be revised as more information
becomes available. A key question is how to interpret
3.1 Key judgements in the forecast
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 64

### Page 68

the recent material increase in longer-term real yields
(see Box A: How Does an Increase in Longer-term Real
Yields Affect Financial Conditions? and Box B:
Developments in the Neutral Rate of Interest). To the
extent that this reflects higher neutral policy rates
globally (and for Australia), the assumed cash rate path
would, by itself, be less restrictive and could weigh less
on activity than projected. On the other hand, if the
recent rise in global yields primarily reflects higher term

premia, that would imply tighter financial conditions
than assumed in the forecast, although this is less likely
to materially change our assessment of restrictiveness,
given the relatively short duration of household and
business liabilities in Australia.
Key judgement #3 – Some of the
current drivers of inflation are
expected to persist and will take
time to moderate.
Some of the upside risks to the inflation outlook
identified in the August Statement have since

materialised and are now incorporated in the central
forecast for inflation. These factors have led to an
upward revision to the inflation outlook over the next
year or so and a delay in the expected timeframe for
inflation to return to the target range. Higher fuel and
other commodity prices from the Middle East conflict
are expected to put sustained upward pressure on
inflation over the coming year as these higher input
costs are passed on to consumer prices to some degree

over time. These cost pressures are expected to ease
over the latter part of the forecast period, contributing to
the decline in inflation. The change to the judgement
around the degree of domestic capacity constraints
implies more persistent inflationary pressures than in the
August forecasts, all else equal (see Chapter 2: Economic
Conditions). That is, we now assess that domestic
capacity pressures have been contributing more to
recent elevated inflation outcomes than we had

previously judged, and from this higher starting point
will contribute a bit more to inflation in the next year.
The elevated level of inflation expectations is also
projected to influence price- and wage-setting
behaviour and boost inflation and wages growth a little
more than previously expected.
We view the risks to the inflation outlook as still skewed
to the upside. Alongside the potential for global cost
pressures to be higher than assumed, it is also possible

we have misjudged the extent of capacity pressures in
the economy and the labour market, and the impact of
the recent increase in inflation expectations in price- and
wage-setting behaviour (see section 3.4 Key risks to the
outlook).

The outlook for growth in Australia’s
major trading partners (MTPs) has
been revised higher over the next
couple of years, but growth is
expected to moderate from its
current strong pace.
The MTP GDP growth forecast has been revised
upwards to 4.0 per cent in 2026, before slowing to
3.5 per cent in 2027 and 3.4 per cent in 2028 as
growth in global AI-related activity and China is
expected to moderate (Graph 3.2). Growth has been
above some estimates of potential and stronger than

expected over the past year, reflecting strong AI-related
activity and, in some economies, continued fiscal
support. These have more than offset the headwinds
from the Middle East conflict. MTP growth is expected to
moderate to around potential over 2027 and 2028.
Overall, we judge that the risks around this outlook for
MTP growth are broadly balanced. The expansion in
global AI-related investment and production may be
more persistent than Consensus forecasters have

factored in, but higher energy prices could also weigh
on activity by more than currently assumed. The outlook
for MTP growth as a whole is broadly in line with the
International Monetary Fund’s projections in the October
2026 World Economic Outlook.
The upward revision to MTP growth is concentrated
in high-income east Asian economies, which are key
exporters of AI-related goods. Strong demand for
semiconductors and other AI-enabling technologies has

supported activity in economies that are deeply
integrated into regional technology supply chains, such
as South Korea and Taiwan. While the outlook for growth
in 2027 has been revised a little higher by external
forecasters, it is still expected to moderate from the
strong pace over the past year towards estimates of
potential growth.

Growth in other advanced economies has also been
more resilient than expected, prompting upward

revisions to near-term forecasts. AI-related investment
and stronger underlying momentum have supported
activity in the United States, while defence spending and
broader resilience across services and investment have
contributed to growth in the euro area. Activity in the
United Kingdom has also held up better than expected,
supported by business investment. These tailwinds
across advanced economies have so far offset some of
the drag to activity from higher energy costs. However,

external forecasters and central banks expect
persistently elevated energy costs to reduce household
purchasing power, raise firms’ costs and increasingly
weigh on consumption and investment. In some
advanced economies, tighter financial conditions will
also weigh on growth.
3.2 The global outlook
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 66

### Page 70

The outlook for GDP growth in China is broadly
unchanged from the August Statement, though
there have been some changes to the expected
timing and composition of growth. GDP growth is
forecast to be 4.6 per cent in 2026, before easing slightly
to 4.5 per cent in 2027 and 4.4 per cent in 2028. The
pick-up in growth in the September quarter is expected
to continue in the near term, given the announcement
of new policy support for infrastructure investment and

signals from authorities that supporting economic
activity will be elevated as a near-term policy priority.
Infrastructure investment is now expected to recover
from late 2026 as this support takes effect, while
structural headwinds are expected to continue weighing
on domestic demand more broadly. The timing and
effectiveness of the policy support remain uncertain,
particularly given high local government debt and a
range of barriers to some local governments

commencing infrastructure projects.
Longer-term government bond yields have risen
across advanced economies, alongside resilient
activity and persistent inflation. The increase appears
to reflect a combination of higher expected near-term
policy rates and higher longer-term real rates, reflecting
a mix of higher term premia and potentially higher
neutral rates. Market-based measures of long-term
inflation expectations remain consistent with central

bank targets. Strong investment and public demand
may be placing upward pressure on real rates, and
expectations of stronger future productivity growth –
including from the adoption of AI – may also be
contributing. Higher public and private debt issuance
and uncertainty about inflation and fiscal trajectories
may also be adding to the compensation investors
require to hold longer-duration bonds (see Box A: How
Does an Increase in Longer-term Real Yields Affect

Financial Conditions? and Box B: Developments in the
Neutral Rate of Interest).
The global inflation outlook has been revised higher,
reflecting an increase in energy prices and
continued resilience in global demand. Consensus
forecasters expect headline inflation to remain above
central bank targets into 2027 in many advanced
economies. Services disinflation has stalled, while core
goods inflation has picked up. The outlook for globally
traded goods inflation has been revised higher,

reflecting higher commodity prices and further strong
increases in export prices for semiconductors and other
AI-related goods across parts of Asia. AI-related price
pressures have not broadened materially beyond
electronics but could become more persistent or spread
to other parts of global supply chains if strong demand
continues to run ahead of the expansion in productive
capacity (see Key risk #2). The further increase in energy
prices since August could add to these upstream cost

pressures, although the extent and timing of
pass-through to consumer prices remains uncertain.
Overall, we continue to judge that the risks to global
inflation remain skewed to the upside.

Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 67

### Page 71

Australian GDP growth is expected
to remain below our estimate of
potential growth throughout the
forecast period.
GDP growth has slowed since the end of 2025 and is
expected to remain subdued over the next couple of
years (Graph 3.3). The expected slowing in growth over
2026 reflects the effects of several headwinds weighing
on private activity, including the reduction in household
disposable incomes from higher inflation, weaker
conditions in the established housing market, and the

tightening in monetary policy, which more than offset
very strong data centre-related investment. Growth is
expected to remain below potential over the forecast
period, reflecting an assumption that financial
conditions remain restrictive (see Key judgement #2).
Consistent with the latest state and federal budgets,
public demand is expected to continue supporting
growth over the forecast period.
The risks to activity appear to be broadly balanced.

Stronger-than-expected global growth and further
strength in AI and data centre-related activity pose
upside risks to domestic growth (see Key risk #2).
However, historically low consumer and business
sentiment could be pointing to the risk that the
combined effect on domestic activity of the Middle East
conflict, the decline in housing prices and the tightening
in monetary policy could be more than assumed (see
Key risk #3).
The level of GDP at end-2028 is expected to be

broadly unchanged from the August forecasts.
Growth is projected to be slightly stronger than
expected in the near term, reflecting a much stronger
outlook for data centre-related investment compared
with the August Statement. Weighing against that is the
higher assumed market path for the cash rate, a weaker
outlook for the housing market and higher oil prices.
These pull growth in the latter part of the forecast period
a little below that expected in the August Statement.

Business investment is expected to increase strongly
and the outlook has been revised up significantly,

largely driven by data centre-related investment.
Business investment was stronger than expected in the
June quarter and firms’ surveyed expectations of future
investment were again revised higher. External
projections for the expected increase in Australia’s data
centre capacity through to 2030 have also continued to
be revised up as the size of the development pipeline
has increased. Our forecast for data centre investment is
in line with recent baseline external projections, but

below projections that assume Australia becomes a
significant regional hub for data centres. There is
considerable uncertainty around the extent of data
centre investment over coming years. This will depend
on a range of factors such as government policy,
community expectations, future demand for AI services
and grid capacity. We continue to expect that much of
this additional investment will be imported (around
70 per cent), limiting the boost to GDP growth. However,

data centre construction, as well as any potential
spillovers to higher investment in energy and utilities
infrastructure, will require domestically sourced labour
and materials. At this stage, most of this investment
pipeline is in New South Wales and Victoria where liaison
information suggests capacity constraints in the
construction sector are somewhat less binding.
3.3 Domestic outlook

### Page 72

At the same time, a higher assumed cash rate path, a
more significant easing in the established housing
market and higher global oil prices are expected to
weigh more on activity than in the August forecasts.
The forecasts are conditioned on a higher market path
for interest rates relative to August. In addition,
established housing prices have continued to fall more
quickly than expected, with external forecasters now
expecting a deeper downturn than assumed a few

months ago. The outlook for oil prices is also much
higher than assumed in August, which will weigh on real
household incomes. Reflecting these factors, the outlook
for household consumption and dwelling investment
has been revised lower, as has the outlook for business
investment unrelated to data centres.
The labour market is expected to
continue easing over the forecast
period, consistent with
below-potential GDP growth.
The unemployment rate is forecast to increase to

5.1 per cent by the end of 2028, which is around
1⁄4 percentage point higher than previously
expected (Graph 3.4). The unemployment rate has risen
by more than expected over recent months, and the
upward revision to forecast relative to the August
Statement largely reflects this higher starting point.
Elevated cost-of-living pressures appear to have
encouraged workers to enter or remain in the labour
force to a greater extent in recent months than was

expected at the time of the August Statement. These
pressures are expected to continue to support
participation for some time. The participation rate is
expected to decline very modestly by the end of 2028 as
easing cyclical pressures are expected to more than
offset the longer-run upward trend in female
participation. A slightly weaker outlook for demand later
in the forecast period is also contributing a little to the
upward revision to the unemployment rate profile by

late 2028.

As in previous forecasts, the
employment-to-population ratio is expected to
decline through to late 2028. Employment growth is

expected to remain broadly stable over the next year, in
line with the leading indicators of labour demand. From
mid-2027, employment growth is expected to be a little
weaker than in the August Statement, reflecting the
weaker outlook for GDP growth later in the forecast
period. The forecast employment-to-population ratio is
similar to the August Statement in the near term, before
declining more quickly to reach a lower level by the end
of the forecast period.

Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 69

### Page 73

The economy is expected to move
into balance in 2027, with spare
capacity then emerging in the
economy over the rest of the forecast
period.
Below-potential output growth and an easing in
labour market conditions are expected to see the
economy move from a position of excess demand to
around balance in 2027, before some spare capacity
emerges. The timing of the return to balance is slightly
later than in the August Statement, as a result of the
upgrade to near-term GDP growth.

However, there is considerable uncertainty around
the assessment of when the economy will return to
balance and the extent of spare capacity that
emerges. The impact of conflict-related costs on
inflation over the period ahead will also make it more
difficult to assess the degree of underlying capacity
pressures in the domestic economy. Our assumption for
potential output growth over the next couple of years
assumes productivity growth will pick up gradually (see

Key risk #4). However, with the level of labour
productivity remaining around the same rate as prior to
the pandemic – implying near-zero productivity growth
on average over that period – continued weak
productivity outcomes may be constraining the ability of
firms to grow their supply capacity without generating
inflationary pressure.
Wages growth is expected to pick up
a little in the near term, then
moderate gradually from 2027 as
labour market conditions ease.

The forecast for year-ended wages growth has been
revised a little higher across the forecast period. In
the near term, nominal wages growth is expected to be
supported by the effect of higher-than-expected
capacity pressures in the first half of the year as well as
the Fair Work Commission’s award wage decision (which
had already been incorporated in the August forecasts).
The influence of both higher-than-expected inflation
outcomes and inflation expectations on wage

negotiations is expected to add further upward pressure
on wages growth relative to the August forecast. Some
large wage increases in enterprise bargaining
agreements are expected to support wages growth in
the near term, although the timing of pay increases for
these agreements may contribute to volatility in
quarterly outcomes, particularly for the public sector.
Wages growth expectations from firms in the RBA’s
liaison program are elevated and suggest little

easing over the year ahead (see Box C: Insights from
Liaison). This is consistent with the central forecast for
wages growth. Growth is expected to moderate from
mid-2027 onwards, reflecting easing labour market
conditions and lower inflation outcomes. Wages growth
is expected to have eased to 2.9 per cent in year-ended
terms by end-2028 (Graph 3.5).

Unit labour cost growth is forecast to
be elevated in the near term, before
easing over the second half of the
forecast period.
Year-ended growth in unit labour costs (ULCs) is
expected to increase in coming quarters, reflecting
weak productivity growth and higher wages growth.
Year-ended growth in ULCs is then expected to
moderate from late 2027 onwards, alongside slowing
nominal wages growth and the pick-up in productivity
growth to its assumed medium-term trend growth rate

of 0.7 per cent per year (Graph 3.6). Nominal ULCs are
the measure of labour costs most relevant for firms’ cost
of production and so for inflation outcomes. Year-ended
ULCs growth is expected to remain above 2.5 per cent –
the rate that is consistent with inflation being
sustainably at target – until late in the forecast period.

The forecast for underlying inflation
has been revised higher over the
next year, mainly owing to higher
conflict-related cost pressures and
higher-than-expected domestic
capacity pressures.
Trimmed mean inflation is expected to remain
above 3 per cent until the end of 2027, easing
gradually to reach 21⁄2 per cent around mid-2028
(Graph 3.7). Year-ended underlying inflation is expected

to remain elevated at a little above 31⁄2 per cent until
mid-2027, reflecting existing capacity pressures in the
economy and the pass-through of higher
conflict-related costs to consumer prices. Elevated
inflation expectations are also contributing to
underlying inflation over the coming year or so. From
mid-2027 onwards, inflation is expected to ease due to
the emergence of spare capacity in the economy and
labour market and as conflict-related cost effects begin

to unwind.

### Page 75

Cost pressures related to the Middle East conflict are
expected to place more upward pressure on
underlying inflation over the year ahead than
previously assumed. A possible re-escalation in the
conflict had been flagged as an upside risk to inflation in
the August Statement. Since then, oil and fuel prices
have increased by much more than had been assumed
and are now assumed to remain at a higher level over
the forecast period. We expect higher global energy

prices will contribute to higher domestic inflationary
pressures in late 2026 and early 2027, even as domestic
activity slows and the labour market eases (Graph 3.8).
The judgement around the degree of pass-through from
global energy prices to domestic prices is broadly similar
to what had been assumed in August. It is consistent
with information from liaison and the Business Outlook
Scenario Survey (BOSS) in September, where firms
reported plans to continue raising prices in response to

past cost increases arising partially from the conflict.
From mid-2027 onwards, we expect cost effects to
unwind in net terms, albeit to a lesser extent than
assumed in the August Statement as oil prices are now
assumed to remain at a higher level than previously
expected and refined margins are assumed to remain
elevated for longer (see Key judgement #1). The balance
of risks to conflict-related inflationary pressures remains
skewed to the upside, particularly as crude oil and

refined fuel prices could remain elevated or rise further
(see Key risk #1).
Relative to the August forecasts, domestic capacity
pressures are also expected to exert a bit more
upward pressure on inflation over the year ahead.
We now judge that stronger-than-expected domestic
capacity pressures explain more of the recent
unexpected strength in underlying inflation (see
Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). Those higher capacity
pressures are also expected to contribute a little more to

inflation over the year ahead. This upward pressure is
expected to ease gradually over the forecast period, as
spare capacity emerges in the economy and the
labour market.

Elevated inflation expectations are expected to

contribute to inflationary pressure over the forecast
period, supporting the pass-through of firms’ costs
to consumer prices. Several measures of short- and
medium-term inflation expectations have increased
alongside the recent increases in fuel prices, and some
measures of longer-term expectations have also drifted
higher (see Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). We judge
that elevated expectations will contribute to higher
realised inflation by supporting expected cost growth,

making firms more willing to pass these higher costs
through to prices, and placing upward pressure on
wages growth. If the conflict in the Middle East is more
protracted than assumed in our baseline forecasts, there
is a risk that inflation expectations may increase further
(see Key risk #1).

Headline inflation has been revised
higher in the near term due to
stronger growth in fuel prices and
higher underlying inflation.
Headline inflation is expected to remain above 3 per
cent until late 2027, which is around three quarters
longer than in the August forecast, and reach the
midpoint of the 2–3 per cent inflation target range a
bit later than expected in August (Graph 3.9). Headline
inflation is expected to peak at 4.5 per cent in the
December quarter this year due to

higher-than-expected fuel prices. The assumed decline
in global oil prices is expected to put downward
pressure on headline inflation from early 2027. Over the
remainder of the forecast period, headline inflation has
been revised higher relative to our August forecasts,
reflecting higher underlying inflation (Graph 3.10).
Headline inflation is expected to reach the midpoint of
the target in mid-2028 as capacity pressures and
conflict-related costs ease.

Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 73

### Page 77

The staff assess that the risks to the domestic inflation
outlook are skewed to the upside, despite becoming
more balanced since the August Statement as some of
the risks identified then have been incorporated into the
central forecast. In August, we identified three main
sources of upside risk to the global and domestic
inflation outlook: a re-escalation of the Middle East
conflict; domestic capacity pressures being greater than
assumed; and the global AI boom generating more of an

inflationary impetus than assumed. Since then, the risk
of a re-escalation of the Middle East conflict has
crystallised and staff have re-evaluated the extent of
domestic capacity constraints; both of which have led to
upward revisions to the central inflation forecast over the
next year. However, there is still uncertainty around the
outlook for global energy prices, the role of inflation
expectations in price and wage dynamics as well as the
impact on inflation from AI activity.

The staff also identified in August that there were upside
risks to global and domestic economic activity from
AI-related demand, but that domestic economic activity
could be weaker if the Middle East conflict and the
decline in housing prices affected the economy by more
than assumed in the central forecast. These remain key
risks to the outlook.
Key risk #1 – High inflation could be
more persistent if energy prices
remain elevated, or if inflation

expectations increase or start to
have a greater bearing on wage- and
price-setting.
We have modelled a scenario where crude oil
remains around US$100 per barrel and refined
product prices remain at elevated levels over the
forecast period. Many external forecasters are revising
higher their outlook for crude oil and refined product
prices, on concerns that some of the key margins of
adjustment in global energy markets that have helped
to cushion the impact of the disruption to oil supply and

global activity are reaching their limits (see Chapter 2:
Economic Conditions and Key judgement #1).
Persistently higher global oil prices would leave
domestic inflation higher for a longer period than in
the central forecast. Higher oil prices would directly
increase fuel prices and firms’ input costs, with the latter
gradually passed through to consumer prices as firms
adjust to persistently higher costs. By contrast, the
central forecast assumes a decline in oil prices, which

starts to ease cost pressures related to the Middle East
conflict from mid-2027, contributing to lower inflation
over time (Graph 3.11). Higher inflation also raises
inflation expectations in the scenario, reinforcing
inflationary pressures over the forecast period. Assuming
that the cash rate and exchange rate follow the same
path that underpins the central forecasts, underlying
inflation would be around 23⁄4 per cent by
end-2028 rather than being around the 21⁄2 per cent.

3.4 Key risks to the outlook
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 74

### Page 78

It is possible that inflation expectations could rise
even further or that inflation expectations provide
greater impetus to wages growth and inflation
through second-round effects. The central forecasts
and scenario assume that the increase in households’
and firms’ inflation expectations do exert some upward
pressure on inflation and wages growth. However, given
households and firms are paying particularly close
attention to inflation, it is possible that inflation

expectations will influence price- and wage-setting
decisions by more than assumed. This heightened
attentiveness to inflation could also make expectations
more persistent and more responsive to realised
inflation. With headline inflation forecast to rise further in
the near term, this increases the risk that inflation
expectations remain higher for longer than assumed in
our central forecast.
Key risk #2 – The global AI demand
boom may continue to drive up

global growth and inflation.
MTP growth may not moderate as expected over the
next few years if AI-demand growth continues at the
same pace. It is possible that growth forecasts could
continue to be upwardly revised by Consensus
forecasters if the current upswing in AI-related
investment (and spillovers associated with the boom) is
more persistent than currently assumed. The rise in
interest rates across the yield curve in many advanced
economies may reflect higher neutral rates – that is,

expectations that demand will prove more resilient and
inflation more persistent than currently assumed. Higher
longer-term real rates may partly reflect expectations of
stronger future productivity growth, including from the
adoption of AI. Further investment in anticipation of
higher productivity could also support demand and add
to capacity pressures over the next few years, while most
of the productivity gains are likely to manifest beyond
the next two years (at least in Australia).

The ramp-up of investment in data centres may also
contribute to capacity and pricing pressures
domestically, particularly by drawing on resources in
the construction industry. These price pressures could
be stronger than currently expected as the forecast
pipeline of investment is worked through, or if
investment proves stronger than expected.
Key risk #3 – Domestic economic
activity could be weaker than
expected.
It is possible that the headwinds affecting

households and businesses over the next year could
have a larger adverse effect than assumed. The
central forecasts assume that higher oil prices, higher
interest rates and lower housing prices all weigh on
domestic economic activity broadly in line with average
historical relationships. Timely indicators of spending
remain consistent with this assumption, with no
evidence of a material slowing in consumption growth
in recent months. However, the very weak levels of

consumer and business sentiment could be pointing to
a more pronounced slowing in household and business
spending growth than assumed.
We have calibrated a downside domestic activity
scenario where there is a larger response in
household and business spending to these
headwinds. There is a range of estimates for the effects
of these headwinds in the literature, highlighting the
uncertainty around their impact on economic activity.
Drawing on this evidence, the scenario uses larger

responses than assumed in the central forecast, thereby
identifying a plausible downside outlook for domestic
activity. In the scenario, firms feel forced to pass on the
conflict-related costs to prices as in the central forecast;
but households respond more cautiously to the
resultant pressures on real income and wealth, reducing
discretionary consumption by more than assumed in the
central forecast. Such an effect on spending might be
compounded by a tightening in borrowing constraints

and incentives for housing investors. The scenario
assumes firms respond to weaker demand and margin
pressure with a sharper pullback on (non-data centre)
investment and hiring. Overall, the level of GDP is around
1.0 per cent lower than the central forecasts
by end-2028.
The larger pullback in household and business
spending results in weaker activity and employment
growth, but only modestly lower inflation than in
the central forecast, assuming interest rates and the

exchange rate follow the same path as the central
forecast. The greater caution exhibited by households
pushes the gross household saving ratio up gradually to
a peak of around 17 per cent, where it remains over the
remainder of the forecast period. The greater reduction
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 75

### Page 79

in hiring pushes the unemployment rate 0.3 percentage
points higher by end-2028, creating more spare capacity
in the domestic economy than in the central forecast
(Graph 3.12). The additional spare capacity in the
economy and the labour market reduces inflationary
pressures somewhat, but the assumption of cost
pass-through by firms limits this effect. Overall, trimmed
mean inflation reaches 21⁄2 per cent only one quarter
earlier than in the central forecast.

Key risk #4 – There remains
considerable uncertainty about the
degree of capacity pressures in the
domestic economy and labour
market.
Despite the recent reassessment, it is possible that

we are still understating the extent of capacity
pressures. While we assess that capacity constraints in
the economy and labour market have been tighter than
previously judged, model-based estimates of the
unemployment rate that is consistent with low and
stable inflation continue to point to greater tightness in
labour market conditions than our central assessment. In
addition, if productivity growth were to remain subdued
over the forecast period, rather than pick up modestly as

assumed, it may suggest that the economy’s supply
capacity would be lower than currently expected. The
implications for inflation would depend on how
households and businesses adjust their spending
and expectations.
Conversely, capacity pressures may be weaker than
expected. The broader suite of full employment
indicators points to somewhat less labour market
tightness than our central assessment. It is also possible
that more of the recent strength in wages growth and

inflation reflects factors other than excess demand, such
as conflict-related cost effects or higher inflation
expectations. In these cases, wages growth and inflation
may moderate more quickly than in the central forecast.
Productivity growth could also recover more quickly
than assumed, including if the anticipated benefits of AI
adoption and other technological developments are
realised sooner.
There is also a risk that the unemployment rate
increases by more than expected if labour supply

remains stronger than assumed. There is a risk that
elevated cost-of-living pressures could support labour
force participation for longer, or to a greater extent, than
assumed. If so, stronger labour supply could result in a
higher unemployment rate than forecast, even if labour
demand evolves broadly as expected.

## Document 321: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-08).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

## Document 333: CTCP Vàng Bạc Đá Quý Phú Nhuận - Cập nhật ĐHCĐ bất thường 21_10_2026 (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Vàng Bạc Đá Quý Phú Nhuận -  Cập nhật ĐHCĐ bất thường 21_10_2026 (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Company)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

Biểu đồ 1. Kết quả kinh doanh theo quý  Biểu đồ 2. Diễn biến doanh thu thuần/ngày và doanh thu bán 
lẻ/ngày theo quý

### Page 2

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

## Document 338: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53 .2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities  in the coming 6 -12 months , 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15 -20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid -to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid -cycle bull market . Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities  within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook  to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) equities , which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Allocation to global equities decrease to 52.5% from 
53.2% for our CIO Foundation Balanced portfolio

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex -UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate  are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low -teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities . We expect the 
segment to generate 10 -15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others  consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years , with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

### Page 3

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High -Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks , MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance  currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advance s in oncology a nd innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks  
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. H ealthcare and 
consumer staples account s for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management  in a mid -
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and p otentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge  
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

Our opportunistic ideas have a more defensive stance 
on average

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund  strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two , and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets , private equity provides  a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, i nfrastructure offers  differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where c ontracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3 -7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell -off caused by AI -
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi -sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010 -2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

## Document 370: Intel出事它卻沒事？營收上升但毛利下降？ 財報解剖_ AMD_ 26年11月

Source PDF: all report pdf/Intel出事它卻沒事？營收上升但毛利下降？ 財報解剖_ AMD_ 26年11月.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 23
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    KP - FOMO 研究院  <fomosoc@substack.com>
Subject: Intel 出事它卻沒事？營收上升但⽑利下降？  財報解剖 : AMD| 26 年 11 ⽉
Date:    3 hours ago
Intel 出事它卻沒事？營收上升但⽑利下降？  財報解剖 : AMD| 26 年 11 ⽉

Intel 出事它卻沒事？營收上升但⽑利
下降？  財報解剖 : AMD| 26 年 11 ⽉
KP@FOMOSOC AND BENITA HSU
NOV 4 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
10 ⽉ 6 ⽇到 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇財報公布前， AMD 的股價從 649.42 美元⼀路跌到 483.90 美
元，不到⼀個⽉摔掉了 165.52 美元（跌幅 25.5% ）。
光是 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇ Intel 發出 PC 賣不動的警訊那天， AMD 單⽇就被拖累⼤跌近
11% 。
財報都還沒出來，市場已經先把最壞的劇本演了⼀遍。

### Page 2

結果 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇盤後財報⼀開牌， AMD 盤後股價⽴刻反彈。
字⾯上的成績單確實漂亮：
第三季總營收年增 45% 來到 134 億美元，第四季營收指引更直接喊到 170 億美
元（年增 66% ），其中「資料中⼼」部⾨第三季營收年增 97% ，幾乎翻倍。

### Page 3

執⾏⻑蘇姿丰更在法說會上預告， 2027 年全公司總營收將再成⻑超過 80% 。
但翻開財報細項，卻會看到兩個耐⼈尋味的反差：
明明營收與獲利都在創紀錄，第三季真正流進⼝袋的「⾃由現⾦流」卻⼤
減了 64% ；⽽且為了綁定⼤客戶所送出的股票（認股權證），還在帳上砸出
了近 11 億美元的業外損失。
這份財報真正要回答的問題是：
財報前跌掉的那 25% ，有多少是市場錯殺？
當 AMD 準備邁向近千億美元的營收規模，這⾨⼤⽣意背後的真實利潤與現⾦
流究竟⻑什麼樣⼦？
接下來，我們就⽤最⽩話的⽅式，把這場轉變背後的財務邏輯⼀步步算清楚。

### Page 4

重點⼀｜ Helios 系統正式出貨：為什
麼資料中⼼賣越多，⽑利率反⽽微降？
1.1 什麼是 Helios ？只賣「核⼼晶⽚」與「設計圖」的⽣意模式
過去買晶⽚，客戶買回去⾃⼰插上伺服器就能⽤。
但現在的 AI 運算太龐⼤，客戶需要的是⼀整座已經幫你把上百顆晶⽚、網路線
與散熱系統全部串聯好的「⼤型機櫃」。
AMD 推出的 Helios ，就是這樣⼀套「整座機櫃的設計藍圖」。
特別的是， AMD ⾃⼰只負責賣機櫃裡最昂貴的核⼼⼤腦（包含 Instinct AI 晶
⽚、 EPYC 處理器、網路晶⽚與軟體），⾄於外⾯的⾦屬機殼、電源供應器與整
機組裝，則交給像 HPE 、 Dell 、 Super Micro 這類品牌伺服器⼤廠與系統夥伴去
出貨並向客戶收費。
這代表 AMD 不必賺利潤微薄的鐵櫃與組裝費，帳⾯認列的營收主要來⾃⾼單價
的核⼼晶⽚⽅案。
不過要注意的是，每⼀顆 AI 晶⽚裡都封裝了向記憶體⼤廠採購的⾼昂 HBM
（⾼頻寬記憶體），這筆硬體採購成本會直接算進 AMD 的銷貨成本裡；再加上

為了搶⼤單所給出的價格優惠，都會影響最終的⽑利率表現。
蘇姿丰在法說會上證實，新⼀代 MI455X 晶⽚已在第三季⼤量出貨，包括
Anthropic 、 Meta 、 Microsoft 、 OpenAI 與 Oracle 都已經讓第⼀批初期的 Helios 系
統上線運作並跑起模型。
到了第四季， AI 晶⽚的營收更將在歷史上⾸度超越傳統伺服器 CPU 。
這也帶動資料中⼼部⾨展現強⼤的賺錢效率：
第三季資料中⼼部⾨營業利益年增 168% 來到 29 億美元，相當於每做 100 美元⽣
意，就有約 34 美元是營業利益（較去年同期的 25 美元⼤幅提升）。
1.2 算⼀筆明細帳：新增資料中⼼營收的五成⽑利秘密
既然核⼼晶⽚這麼熱銷，為什麼 AMD 預估第四季全公司的⽑利率會從 56% 微
幅降到 55% ？

### Page 5

道理很像⼀家便當店接到了⼤型企業的團膳⼤單：
雖然為了搶下⼤單，每個便當的單價壓低了⼀點、⾷材成本也⾼，讓平均⽑利
率微幅下降，但因為⼀次賣出好幾千個，收銀機進帳的總獲利反⽽迎來⼤爆
發。
我們可以把第四季的財測數字拆開來，算算看 AMD 多賣的這批資料中⼼訂單
到底賺多少⽑利：
先算資料中⼼多賣了多少錢：
第四季總營收預計為  170 億美元，⽐第三季多出約  36 億美元。
但別忘了，第三季貢獻  37 億美元的「 PC 與電競部⾨」，第四季預計會衰退超
過  20% （⾄少少掉  7.4 億美元）。
⼀邊少賺、⼀邊還要讓總營收⼤增，算下來資料中⼼第四季營收必須單季暴增
約  43 億美元，相當於⼀個季度就⻑⼤了  50% 。

### Page 6

再算這批新增訂單的⽑利率：
第四季預估總⽑利約  93.5 億美元（ 170 億  × 55% ），⽐第三季多出  18.7 億美
元。
假設少掉的那  7.4 億美元  PC 業務⽑利率⼤約是  50% （此為推算假設），回推那
43 億美元新增的資料中⼼營收，每多賣  100 美元⼤約只能賺到  50 美元的⽑利
（邊際⽑利率粗估約  50% ）。
NVIDIA 整體的平均⽑利率⻑期⾼達 70% 以上，⽽ AMD 第四季度新增資料
中⼼營收（包含 AI 晶⽚與伺服器 CPU ）的邊際⽑利率粗估約落在 50% 左
右。
這組數字除了反映⾼昂的 HBM 記憶體成本外，也把「 AMD ⽤價格折扣與送
股票換取市占率」變成了⼀個具體可追蹤的財務指標。
當然，因為單季多賣了 43 億美元，規模放⼤的威⼒依然驚⼈，第四季全公司的
營業利益預計將從第三季的 39 億美元⼀路跳升⾄約 56 億美元。
重點⼆｜管理層喊出 2027 成⻑破
80% ：要達標，你得相信什麼？
2.1 短短 90 天內，再度⼤幅調⾼明年⽬標

⽐起第三季成績單，這場法說會真正的重頭戲，是管理層⼤幅調⾼了 2027 年的
成⻑⽬標。
蘇姿丰表⽰，過去 90 天內多家雲端巨頭擴⼤了採購計畫。
對⽐ 8 ⽉份法說會時管理層預估「明年伺服器 CPU 成⻑超過 70% 」，這次直接上
修為「明年伺服器 CPU 營收將接近翻倍」，資料中⼼ AI 晶⽚營收更將「成⻑遠
超過兩倍（ grow significantly more than twofold ）」，帶動資料中⼼佔 2027 年全公
司總營收達到 80% 以上。

### Page 7

更重要的是，管理層指出 2027 年伺服器 CPU 營收翻倍的動能來⾃三⼤塊：
傳統雲端與企業端汰換、 AI 代理（ Agentic AI ）⼯作負載，以及 AI 叢集的主節
點（ Head Nodes ）。
隨著新⼀代 EPYC Venice 處理器在第三季開始出貨（合作夥伴承諾開發的平台
數超過 240 個，⽐上⼀代多出兩成），這波 CPU 成⻑主要是由「出貨量增加」與
「⾼核⼼數產品組合」所驅動，⽽⾮單純靠漲價，這意味著企業與雲端升級
CPU 的需求⽐市場想像得更加紮實。
2.2 邁向 940 億美元的算術題：真正的上限在⼯廠產能
「 2027 年總營收成⻑超過 80% 」換算成⾦額有多⼤？
2026 年全年營收基準： 2026 年前三季營收加上第四季預估的  170 億美
元，今年全年總營收約為  521 億美元。

### Page 8

2027 年營收⽬標： 明年再成⻑超過  80% ，代表  2027 全年營收將⼀舉突破
939 億美元。這個數字已經追平了外資  Bernstein 先前最樂觀的預估（ 938
億美元），更超越了  Morgan Stanley 的預期（ 889 億美元）。
但要做到近 940 億美元並不容易。
就算把第四季創紀錄的 170 億美元直接乘以 4 個季度，⼀年也才 680 億美元，中
間還有 260 億美元的缺⼝。
這代表在 2027 年裡， AMD 每⼀季營收平均都得⽐上⼀季再⻑⼤ 13% 到 14% ，且
到了 2027 年第四季，單季營收必須衝上約 280 億美元。
蘇姿丰在問答環節坦⾔⽬前「訂單需求⼤於供給」。這說明 2027 年營收能不
能衝上 940 億美元，關鍵在於 AMD 能從供應商⼿上搶到多少⾼階記憶體與
晶⽚封裝產能。
重點三｜買晶⽚送股票：拆解「認股權
證」的代價與財報怪象
3.1 算算看送股票的代價： Meta 的領股條件⽐ OpenAI 更寬鬆
為了吸引雲端巨頭採⽤⾃家產品， AMD 先前與 OpenAI 及 Meta 簽署協議，分別

給予最⾼ 1.6 億股（合計 3.2 億股）、每股只需要花 0.01 美元就能認購的「附條
件認股權證」。
為什麼要送這麼⼤禮？
因為⼤客戶從 NVIDIA 換成 AMD ，得派⼤批⼯程師重寫軟體、重新設計機房，
轉換成本極⾼。
但仔細對⽐兩家巨頭的合約細節，會發現條件⼤不相同：
OpenAI （綁定採購量＋股價⾨檻）：
OpenAI 的  1.6 億股權證採分批解鎖，除了要買夠晶⽚，最後⼀批還規定要等
AMD 股價漲到  600 美元才能領⾛。
如果  OpenAI 最終全數領滿，這批股票的市值⾄少⾼達  960 億美元（ 1.6 億股  ×
600 美元）。
Meta （只綁採購與出貨⾥程碑，無股價⾨檻）：

### Page 9

Meta 的條件更為寬鬆。同樣最多可領  1.6 億股（搭配  5 年最⾼  600 億美元的採
購承諾），但只看採購量與出貨⾥程碑，沒有設定股價⾨檻。
換句話說，就算  AMD 股價原地踏步，只要  Meta 買夠多晶⽚，股票⼀樣拿得
到。
⽤⽬前的股數換算，兩家巨頭最多各⾃可以拿到 AMD ⼤約⼀成的股份（若 3.2
億股全數發出，現有股東的持股⽐例將被稀釋約 16.0% ）。
這筆交易的本質，就是拿原有股東的股權，去補貼⼤客戶轉換平台的建置成
本。
3.2 財報上的反直覺怪象：五成⽑利背後的「折價券」與業外損
失
你可能會好奇，為什麼第三季 AMD 官⽅財報（ GAAP ）的每股盈餘只有 1.46 美
元，但調整後（ Non-GAAP ）卻⾼達 2.09 美元，中間差了 0.63 美元？
翻開財報調節表，除了常⾒的員⼯股票薪酬（ 5.23 億美元）與併購攤銷（ 5.34
億美元）以及所得稅調整的抵銷（約負 10.73 億美元，即每股抵銷 0.64 美元）
之外，單⼀最⼤的⼀塊調整項，就來⾃這批承諾送給客戶的認股權證：

### Page 10

當作折價券直接扣營收（拉低⽑利率的隱形推⼿）：
根據會計規則，當  AMD 開始向  OpenAI 與  Meta 出貨時，送出去的權證價值會
按⽐例當作給客戶的「銷售折扣（營收折讓）」，直接從營收裡扣掉。
這正好解開了重點⼀的謎團：為什麼第四季新增資料中⼼營收的邊際⽑利率只
有  50% 左右？
因為裡⾯有⼀部分利潤，其實就是以「送股票當折價券」的形式讓利給了⼤客
戶。
每季重新計價的「⽋條」損失：
在客戶還沒真正把權證換成股票之前，會計規則把這批承諾要給的股票視為
AMD ⽋客戶的「負債（⽋條）」，⽽且每⼀季都要按照最新股價與出貨進度重
新計算這張⽋條值多少錢。
第三季因為  AMD 股價上漲、出貨順利，這張⽋條跟著變貴了，導致  AMD 必
須在官⽅損益表上認列⾼達  10.97 億美元（每股  0.66 美元）的業外帳⾯損失。

### Page 11

這會造成⼀個⾮常反直覺的財報現象：未來只要 AMD 晶⽚賣得越好、股價
漲得越⾼，官⽅ GAAP 損益表上的「帳⾯損失」反⽽會跟著變⼤。
（⾄於實際的股數增加與稀釋，財務⻑表⽰要等 2027 年客戶陸續達成出貨
條件並換成股票時才會發⽣。）
3.3 怎麼判斷客戶是不是衝著送股票來的？把名單分成兩組看
既然送股票的代價這麼⾼，我們該怎麼確認客戶買 AMD 是因為產品真的好
⽤？最實際的做法，是把⼤客戶分成兩組來追蹤：
有附帶條件的客戶： OpenAI 與  Meta （拿到認股權證，且  AMD 近期也宣
布投資  Meta 10 億美元），以及  Anthropic （合約中包含  AMD 反過來投資
Anthropic 的承諾）。
純商業採購的客戶（無附帶條件）： Microsoft 、 Oracle ，以及這次新宣
布、透過  HPE 出貨給雲端服務商  Vultr 的  12 億美元  Helios 機櫃⼤單。

### Page 12

追蹤 Microsoft 、 Oracle 與 Vultr 這類「不拿免費股票也願意掏錢」的客戶訂單⽐
重，會是接下來檢驗 AMD 真實產品定價權最直接的溫度計。
重點四｜同樣遇到 PC 賣不動，為什麼
AMD 沒像 Intel ⼀樣暴雷？
4.1 伺服器 CPU 都在⼤賺，差別在於「包袱有多重」
10 ⽉底 Intel 發出財報警訊時，市場⼀度擔⼼ AMD 也會跟著遭殃。
但其實翻開 Intel 財報， Intel 第三季伺服器 CPU 出貨量年增了 10% ，平均售價更
⼤漲 54% ，帶動資料中⼼營收⼤增 78% 來到 73 億美元。
Intel 真正的痛點有兩個：

### Page 13

第⼀， PC 業務仍佔它總營收將近⼀半（ 81 億美元），當 PC 買氣下滑，⽴刻把第
四季總營收指引拖累到 155 億美元；
第⼆， Intel ⾃⼰養了昂貴的晶圓廠，不管⼯廠有沒有訂單，每季都得付龐⼤的
機器折舊與維護費，⼀旦 PC 銷量變少，產能利⽤率下滑的⼯廠就會嚴重吃掉
利潤。
4.2 AMD 的兩⼤優勢：逾六成營收靠資料中⼼、晶圓產能靈活
調度
AMD 同樣沒躲過記憶體漲價與 PC 買氣降溫。
第三季 PC 與電競部⾨營收年減 8% 來到 37 億美元，第四季更預計衰退超過
20% ；管理層甚⾄在法說會上坦⾔，因為記憶體與零組件漲價壓抑終端買氣，
內部在規劃 2027 年財務⽬標時，已經直接假設「 2027 年全球 PC 出貨量將出現
雙位數百分⽐（ 10% 以上）的衰退」。

### Page 14

但即便把這麼悲觀的 PC 假設放進模型裡， AMD 卻依然能交出明年總營收成⻑
破 80% 的超預期指引，關鍵就在於兩⼤體質差異：
資料中⼼已經⻑成絕對主⼒：
AMD 的資料中⼼營收佔⽐已從去年的  47% 拉升到⽬前的  64% （第四季將逼近
75% ，明年更將超過  80% ），靠著伺服器  CPU 與  AI 晶⽚雙引擎，輕鬆填補了
PC 的缺⼝。
把台積電晶圓轉去做⾼⽑利產品：
雖然  AMD 對台積電也有產能採購承諾，但因為台積電的⾼階晶圓產能⾮常吃
緊，當  PC 需求變弱時，蘇姿丰在法說會上指出， AMD 剛好可以把原本要做
PC 的晶圓與載板封裝產能，靈活調度去做⽑利更⾼、供不應求的資料中⼼產
品。
Intel 則剛好相反， PC ⼀弱，⾃家⼯廠的產能利⽤率就跟著掉。

### Page 15

同樣遇到  PC 逆⾵， AMD 是在升級產品組合， Intel 則是被迫吸收龐⼤的⼯
廠固定成本。
重點五｜搶產能與拼軟體：記憶體漲價
下， ROCm 10 扮演什麼⾓⾊？
5.1 記憶體⼤廠開始漲價，成本誰來吸收？
前⾯提到，決定 AMD 2027 年能否衝上 940 億美元的關鍵在於供應鏈產能。
每⼀顆 AI 晶⽚裡都必須搭配⾼頻寬記憶體（ HBM ），⽽記憶體⼤廠 SK hynix 已
經宣布與 14 家⼤客戶簽下⻑約，把 2027 到 2028 年過半的產能都預售光了，還
加⼊了隨市場⾏情調漲價格的條款。
當上游產能被搶光，記憶體廠⾃然掌握了漲價話語權。
蘇姿丰在法說會上坦承零組件確實⾯臨漲價壓⼒，⽬前正與客戶協商「共同分
攤成本」。
這筆多出來的硬體成本，最終有多少能轉嫁給雲端巨頭、有多少得由 AMD ⾃
⼰吸收，將直接影響明年的⽑利率表現。
5.2 為什麼軟體是破局關鍵？幫客戶省下「⼯程師的加班費」
在硬體成本變貴的背景下， AMD 推出最新 ROCm 10 軟體系統的重要性就更加

凸顯了。
我們站在雲端客戶的⾓度想⼀想：
買晶⽚硬體只是第⼀步。如果你買 AMD 的晶⽚省了 20% 硬體費，卻得派幾百
名⾼薪⼯程師花三個⽉改寫程式、解決當機問題才能讓機房順利運轉，那浪費
掉的時間與⼯程師薪⽔，早就把省下的硬體錢吃光了。
ROCm 10 主打讓⼤型機櫃買回去就能快速啟動、⾃動調校效能，⽬的就是把
客戶的「使⽤⾨檻與⿇煩程度」降到最低。
這也是為什麼 AMD 在第三季同步出⼿完成了兩筆關鍵收購：
⼀是收購由 AI 視覺教⺟李⾶⾶創⽴的空間智慧新創 World Labs ，並延攬李⾶⾶
直接出任 AMD ⾸席科學家，從最前沿的模型研發端開始與硬體及 ROCm 軟體
深度綁定；

### Page 16

⼆是收購客製化極速推論晶⽚新創 Taalas ，強化超低延遲的推論能⼒。
只要軟體與模型⽣態系越好⽤、幫客戶省下的⼯程師時間與維護成本越
多， AMD 就越不需要靠硬體殺價或送股票來搶⽣意。
重點六｜帳⾯⼤賺 35 億，為什麼進⼝
袋的現⾦卻少掉六成？
6.1 拆解 33 億美元的「做⽣意墊款」
第三季 AMD 在調整後的損益表上⼤賺了 34.7 億美元，但真正流進公司⼝袋、
可以⾃由動⽤的現⾦（⾃由現⾦流）卻只有 5.54 億美元。
無論是跟上⼀季的 15.58 億美元、還是去年同期的 15.30 億美元相⽐，都⼤幅縮
⽔了 64% 。

### Page 17

第三季買設備與蓋廠房的資本⽀出為 6.48 億美元（⽐第⼆季的 8.08 億美元還
少），真正把現⾦吸⾛的，是⾼達 33 億美元的「⽇常做⽣意墊款」（營運資⾦淨
流出）。
要做近千億美元的⼤⽣意，在收到客戶貨款前， AMD 得先在三個地⽅⾃掏腰包
墊錢：
買原料囤貨（多壓了近  30 億美元）：
倉庫裡的存貨總額攀升到  115 億美元（平均要放  169 天才能賣出，⽐上季的
145 天更久），主要是為了第四季與明年的  MI455 晶⽚及  Helios 系統提前備
料。
付訂⾦鎖產能（多付了約  10 億美元）：
預付款項增加了  10 億美元。對照前⾯  SK hynix 要求簽⻑約鎖產能，這筆錢很
⼤⼀部分就是為了幫  2027 年搶下記憶體與封裝產能所付出的現⾦訂⾦。
客戶拿了貨還沒付錢（應收帳款多了約  19 億美元）：
貨已經出給客戶並算進營收了，但客戶平均要  63 天後才會把錢匯過來（⽐上
季的  57 天拉⻑）。
值得注意的是，現在買  AI 晶⽚的除了  Microsoft 、 Meta 這種現⾦滿⼿的巨頭

外，也包含⼀部分還在⼤量燒錢的  AI 新創實驗室，未來這筆帳款能不能準時
收回，值得持續追蹤。

### Page 18

把上⾯三項加起來，流出⾦額⾼達約 59 億美元；扣掉 AMD ⾃⼰延後付給供應
商的貨款（應付帳款與其他負債增加約 26 億美元）之後，算下來的營運資⾦淨
流出就是 33 億美元。
6.2 公司不缺錢，但算估值時要把「墊款成本」算進去
財務⻑已經預告，為了⽀撐明年的⾼成⻑， 2027 年還是得繼續掏現⾦買庫存、
鎖產能。
不過⼤家不⽤擔⼼ AMD 會資⾦周轉不靈。
截⾄第三季末， AMD 帳上躺著⾼達 158 億美元的現⾦與短期投資，⽽借款（總
債務）只有 71 億美元（其中包含今年 8 ⽉為了提前儲備營運糧草所發⾏的 47.5
億美元公司債），財務家底⾮常厚實。

### Page 19

損益表上的 EPS 告訴你東⻄賣掉後帳⾯賺多少；⽽⾃由現⾦流則誠實地提醒
我們，為了撐起這場千億美元的營收爆發， AMD 必須先掏出多少現⾦去墊
款備料。
重點七｜從⼤跌 25% 到盤後反彈：現
在約 510 美元的 AMD 到底貴不貴？
7.1 現在的股價到底在反映什麼？
財報公布前，外資對 AMD 2027 年 EPS 的預估平均約 15.7 美元。以這個數字計
算， 10 ⽉初 649.42 美元的股價約是 41.4 倍本益⽐，財報前跌到 483.90 美元時約
是 30.8 倍。

### Page 20

現在管理層喊出 2027 年營收成⻑超過 80% ，我們重算⼀次：
營收與⽑利： 營收抓  940 億美元，⽑利率約  54% ，總⽑利約  508 億美
元。
營業費⽤給兩種假設： 營收成⻑  80% ，費⽤不必跟著漲這麼多，但  AMD
每年都要推新⼀代晶⽚、加碼軟體投資，費⽤也不會原地不動。樂觀⼀點
抓  175 億美元（成⻑約  25% ），保守⼀點抓  190 億美元（成⻑約  35% ）。
淨利： 扣掉  13% 稅率，全年淨利約落在  276 億到  289 億美元。
每股盈餘： 如果把明年部分解鎖的客戶權證算進去（假設股數增加到約
17.6 億股）， EPS ⼤約是  15.7 到  16.4 美元。
有趣的是，費⽤抓 190 億美元時算出的 15.7 美元，幾乎和財報前的市場共識⼀
模⼀樣。 Bernstein 先前預估營收 938 億美元， EPS 也只有 15.82 美元。
營收⽬標往上調了⼀⼤截，每股盈餘卻沒跟著動多少。多出來的⽣意，被五成
左右的邊際⽑利、送出去的股票和增加的費⽤分掉了。

### Page 21

拿 15.7 到 16.4 美元對照盤後約 510 美元的股價，遠期本益⽐⼤約是 31 到 32.5
倍。
本益⽐之外，還有⼀個數字要⼀起看。
現在 AMD 市值約 8,540 億美元，如果 2027 年淨利 280 億美元能有⼋成變成⾃由
現⾦流，股價約是⾃由現⾦流的 38 倍；如果只有五成，就變成 61 倍。第三季
這個⽐例只有 16% 。
所以現在的 510 美元，⼤致是「相信 940 億美元營收會達標，也相信明年的
現⾦會跟著回來」。
營收和現⾦這兩件事少了任何⼀件，現價都不算便宜。
7.2 給讀者的三個估值參考座標

### Page 22

AMD 現在訂單⽐產能多，所以明年真正的變數是能拿到多少記憶體與封裝產
能、⽤什麼成本出貨。
以下三個情境都⽤上⾯同⼀套公式計算，再搭配不同的本益⽐。（註：此為測
試獲利與本益⽐變化的算術情境，並⾮⽬標價預測。）
保守情境（產能卡關、記憶體漲價吃掉⽑利）： 營收  880 億美元、⽑利率
53.5% 、費⽤  185 億美元， EPS 約  14.3 美元。客戶買得少，解鎖的權證也
少，稀釋較輕。乘以  25 倍，約  357 美元。
基準情境（產能⼤致到位、達標  940 億美元）： EPS 約  15.7 到  16.4 美
元，乘以  31 倍，約  487 到  510 美元，⼤致就是⽬前的盤後股價。
樂觀情境（拿到更多產能、成本成功轉嫁給客戶）： 營收  1,000 億美元、
⽑利率  56% 、費⽤  180 億美元， EPS 約  18.6 美元。這時客戶買得多，解
鎖的權證也多，股數假設約  17.8 億股。乘以  35 倍，約  650 美元，剛好回
到  10 ⽉初的⾼點。

樂觀情境裡有個⼩諷刺： AMD 賣得越好，送出去的股票就越多。
結論
回頭看財報前那 25% （約 165 美元）的跌幅，我們可以⽤⽂章裡的數字拆成兩
塊來算。
第⼀塊是 PC 恐慌。
事實上，管理層在給出「明年總營收成⻑破 80% 」的⽬標時，內部早就已經把
「 2027 年全球 PC 出貨量衰退雙位數百分⽐」的悲觀預期算進去了。
就算我們假設 2027 年 PC 與電競部⾨再多衰退兩成，少掉的營收⼤約是 24 到 28
億美元（以第四季單季約 30 億美元推算），在 50% ⽑利率與 13% 稅率假設下，
換算成 EPS ⼤約只影響 0.7 美元。
⽤ 30 倍本益⽐計算，⼤概只值 20 美元出頭的股價；就算⽤財報前 41 倍的本益
⽐來算，也不到 30 美元。
Intel 暴雷那天 AMD 單⽇跌掉近 11% ，跌幅遠遠超過 PC 業務真正能造成的傷
害。
第⼆塊是本益⽐從 41 倍回到 30 倍左右，這⼀段反⽽有它的道理。

### Page 23

要讓  649 美元的股價在 30 倍本益⽐下站得住腳， AMD 2027 年的 EPS 得做到
21.6 美元左右；⽤同樣的營收與費⽤往回推，全公司⽑利率必須拉⾼到 63% 上
下。
但這份財報透露的⽅向剛好相反：新增資料中⼼營收的邊際⽑利率⼤約五成、
送給 OpenAI 與 Meta 的權證會稀釋股本並折減營收，搶產能⼜持續壓著現⾦
流。
營收的⾼成⻑已經被證實了，但賺錢的品質還需要時間證明。
⽽這被殺的估值倍數未來能不能再賺回來，就要看接下來這  4 個核⼼指
標：
1. Q4 資料中⼼爆發⼒： 第四季資料中⼼營收能否如期單季⼤增  50% （多賣
約  43 億美元）？
2. ⽑利率防線： 在邊際⽑利約  50% 的新訂單放量、權證折扣攤提，以及記
憶體漲價的分攤協商下，全公司⽑利率能否穩守  55% ？
3. 純商業訂單的拓展： Microsoft 、 Oracle 與  Vultr 這類「不拿免費股票」的
標準訂單，佔⽐能否持續擴⼤？
4. 現⾦回收的速度： 壓在倉庫裡的  115 億美元存貨與  19 億美元新增應收帳

款，明年能否順利變成現⾦流回⼝袋？
AMD 已經向市場證明了⾃⼰搶下近千億營收規模的實⼒；⽽在下⼀個階段，股
價能不能重回前⾼，將取決於這場盛宴過後，最終有多少利潤能真正轉化為⾃
由現⾦流，留進股東的⼝袋裡。

天然資源受地緣擾動的展望
分析師／林珍安
研究範疇／能源/ 原物料
天然資源產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/12
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《地緣風險變數使油價短期仍偏上》
OPEC+決議11月產出配額維持 9月規模，預期目前
配額將持續至今年年底 ，下一輪恢復最後一套 200
萬桶/日的額外減產措施將在明年視市況調整。依市
場推估，中東區域海運原油及成品油運輸出口量9月
底曾回至接近九成 ，受荷姆茲海峽油輪襲擊事件增
加，伊朗革命衛隊預告管理範圍擴大 ，使原油運輸
開始下降 ，但曼德海峽因沙國駐葉門大使稱已清除
胡塞武裝勢力 ，預期曼德海峽運輸量將不受影響 。
但受轉運延長運輸時間 、避開受制裁的原油採購 ，
加上搶補低庫存 ，市場追逐現貨使布油呈現大幅現

貨溢價，七國集團(G7)雖計畫在未來四個月內釋放
總計原油與柴油戰略儲備各 0.5億桶，總計1億桶，
但預計佔每天原油用量低 ，僅短期壓抑油價 ，主要
重點仍在美伊談判、美國向中東增兵、伊朗襲船，
市場投資展望

《天然氣價格：美持平或略低於去年 ，歐洲偏上行 》

續增至11月中，預估今年冬天暖氣度日數將持平去
年 ， 並 比 過 去 五 個 冬 季 的 均 值 低1% ， 預估美國
4Q26天然氣價格將因季節性走升，但持平或略低於
去年。卡達能源LNG船已出口 ，但非往歐洲 ，歐洲
儲氣量雖拉升 ，但僅達 70%，遠低於過往 ，TTF價
格須優於亞洲以吸引運往亞洲的 LNG轉往歐洲 ，加

上中東地緣緊張，預期4Q26 TTF價格仍有上行風險。

《釋儲及放寬制裁增加柴油恐使煉油利潤見頂》
受歐美老舊煉油廠關廠 ，煉油產能因俄烏 、中東戰
事受損，估計全球煉油廠開工率年減近 800萬桶/日，
加上中、俄禁止柴油出口 ，使柴油供應比原油更緊
張。美國柴油庫存低於五年區間下緣 ，柴油價格創
高，因美未禁止柴油出口 ，柴油利潤保持強勁 。但
美協調G7國家釋出柴油儲備為期四個月 ，達成美俄

柴油供應協議增加供給 ，雖粗略換算為短期每日供
給增加不到全球 1%，預期對柴油價格壓力影響僅
5%內，明年隨設備漸修復、地緣風險降低後，才緩
降，但美國超額柴油利潤恐見頂。
《4Q26能源類股較看好綜合油企》
預估美綜合油企 4Q26受惠油價高於預期 、LNG出
口增加，柴油高裂解價差 ，但進入汽油需求淡季 ，
獲利季比下滑 ，但年比仍大幅成長 ，現金流仍強大 ，
維持資本紀律及股東回報 。雖成品油供應仍較原油
吃緊，但煉油商超額利潤恐見頂 ，較看好綜合油企 。

《預期金價短期不利因素多，但中期緩升》
近月金價歷經 美國通膨及經濟成長 ，美國FED採取
預防管理式升息一碼 ，認為年底前仍有升息一碼空
間，但受就業降溫及通膨低於預期 ，市場降低10月
升息預期 ，加上法國國債被拋售蔓延全球債市使美
長期公債殖利率及美元走強 、黃金淨多頭部位下降 ，
壓低本月金價下跌 ，也跌破季線 。預期短線金價受
債券殖利率飆升 、實質利率走升 及美元走強 相對不
利金價走勢 ，震盪放大。但若美債收益率回落 、擔
憂債務可持續性 、分散交易對手風險及多元化資產
配置，中國人行連續23個月購金，並加大購金步伐 ，
3Q26黃金ETF呈現創紀錄淨買超 ，預期3800-4000
美元有撐，維持金價中期緩步上行。

## Document 559: 中国10月CPI同比增0.7%，数据存储设备涨45.4%，中国10月PPI同比增5.7%，创2022年6月以来最高

Source PDF: all report pdf/中国10月CPI同比增0.7%，数据存储设备涨45.4%，中国10月PPI同比增5.7%，创2022年6月以来最高.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

中国国家统计局城市司⾸席统计师董莉娟指出，能源相关⾏业价格上涨是推动 PPI ⾛⾼的主
要动⼒。煤炭开采和洗选业同⽐上涨 36.6% ，⽯油和天然⽓开采业、⽯油煤炭及其他燃料
加⼯业分别上涨 35.0% 和 29.9% ，化学原料和化学制品制造业上涨 16.4% 。上述 9 个主要上
涨⾏业合计拉动 PPI 同⽐上涨约 6.14 个百分点，较上⽉增加 0.64 个百分点。
摘要：
国家统计局周⼀（ 11 ⽉ 9 ⽇）公布数据显⽰， 10 ⽉全国居⺠消费价格指数（ CPI ）同⽐上涨
0.7% ，涨幅较上⽉收窄 0.2 个百分点，环⽐持平。
与此同时，⼯业⽣产者出⼚价格指数（ PPI ）同⽐上涨 5.7% ，涨幅较上⽉扩⼤ 0.7 个百分点，延
续上⾏势头。国家统计局城市司⾸席统计师董莉娟指出，能源相关⾏业价格上涨是推动 PPI ⾛⾼
的主要动⼒，能源价格传导效应显著。
国家统计局城市司⾸席统计师董莉娟解读 2026 年 10 ⽉份 CPI 和 PPI 数据：
其中，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格分别上涨 45.4% 、 29.3% 、 23.0% 和

16.7% ，四项合计影响 CPI 同⽐上涨约 0.26 个百分点。
数据显⽰，煤炭开采和洗选业同⽐上涨 36.6% ，⽯油和天然⽓开采业、⽯油煤炭及其他燃料加⼯
业分别上涨 35.0% 和 29.9% ，化学原料和化学制品制造业上涨 16.4% 。上述 9 个主要上涨⾏业合
计拉动 PPI 同⽐上涨约 6.14 个百分点，较上⽉增加 0.64 个百分点。

### Page 2

10 ⽉份，居⺠消费价格指数（ CPI ）环⽐持平；同⽐上涨 0.7% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回落 0.2 个百分点；
扣除⻝品和能源价格的核⼼ CPI 同⽐上涨 0.8% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回落 0.2 个百分点。⼯业⽣产者出⼚
价格指数（ PPI ）环⽐上涨 0.7% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回落 0.5 个百分点；同⽐上涨 5.7% ，涨幅⽐上⽉扩
⼤ 0.7 个百分点。
⼀、 CPI 同⽐继续上涨
从环⽐看，全国 CPI 由上⽉上涨 0.2% 转为持平。其中，⼯业消费品价格上涨 0.3% ，涨幅⽐上⽉
回落 0.4 个百分点，影响 CPI 环⽐上涨约 0.10 个百分点。⼯业消费品中，受国际市场价格变动影
响，国内汽油价格上涨 4.2% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回落 0.7 个百分点，影响 CPI 环⽐上涨约 0.14 个百分
点；⻩⾦饰品价格下降 5.9% ，降幅⽐上⽉扩⼤ 3.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环⽐下降约 0.03 个百分
点。服务价格由上⽉下降 0.2% 转为上涨 0.1% ，影响 CPI 环⽐上涨约 0.04 个百分点。服务中，国

庆假期出⾏⼈数增加，宾馆住宿、⻜机票和旅⾏社收费分别上涨 6.5% 、 3.3% 和 2.4% ，合计影响
CPI 环⽐上涨约 0.04 个百分点。⻝品价格由上⽉上涨 0.4% 转为下降 1.0% ，影响 CPI 环⽐下降约
0.17 个百分点。⻝品中，⽓候条件适宜，鸡蛋、鲜菜和鲜果价格分别下降 6.3% 、 5.8% 和 0.6% ，
合计影响 CPI 环⽐下降约 0.13 个百分点；市场供应充⾜，⽔产品和猪⾁价格分别下降 1.3% 和
1.0% ，合计影响 CPI 环⽐下降约 0.04 个百分点；⽜⾁和⽺⾁价格分别上涨 1.6% 和 0.8% ，涨幅均
有回落。
从同⽐看，全国 CPI 上涨 0.7% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回落 0.2 个百分点。其中，汽油价格上涨 22.4% ，涨
幅⽐上⽉扩⼤ 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 同⽐上涨约 0.63 个百分点，带动能源价格涨幅由上⽉的
7.3% 扩⼤⾄本⽉的 9.9% 。扣除能源的⼯业消费品价格上涨 1.2% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回落 0.4 个百分

点，影响 CPI 同⽐上涨约 0.30 个百分点。其中，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机
价格分别上涨 45.4% 、 29.3% 、 23.0% 和 16.7% ，四项合计影响 CPI 同⽐上涨约 0.26 个百分点；

医疗服务价格上涨 3.6% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回落 0.4 个百分点；出⾏服务价格上涨 1.5% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回

百分点。⻝品中，鲜菜、猪⾁和鲜果价格分别下降 16.9% 、 8.6% 和 3.8% ，合计影响 CPI 同⽐下

个百分点；⽜⾁和⽺⾁价格分别上涨 8.7% 和 5.8% ，合计影响 CPI 同⽐上涨约 0.09 个百分点。
⼆、 PPI 环⽐涨幅回落
从环⽐看，全国 PPI 上涨 0.7% ，涨幅⽐上⽉回落 0.5 个百分点。本⽉ PPI 环⽐运⾏的主要特点：⼀
是输⼊性和季节性因素影响主要能源相关⾏业价格上涨。国际能源价格上⾏带动国内⽯油和天然
⽓开采业、煤炭开采和洗选业价格分别上涨 6.0% 和 2.7% ；受上游基础能源价格传导和迎峰度冬

加⼯、燃⽓⽣产和供应业价格分别上涨 6.8% 、 6.8% 、 3.2% 和 1.5% ， 6 个⾏业合计影响 PPI 环⽐
上涨约 0.61 个百分点，超过总涨幅的⼋成。⼆是产能治理有序推进、⼈⼯智能快速发展带动部分
原材料及装备制造⾏业价格上⾏。部分⾏业产能有序调整，供求关系有所改善，⾮⾦属矿物制品

业价格上涨 0.8% ，汽⻋制造业价格在连续下降 3 个⽉后转为持平；⼈⼯智能持续赋能产业转型带

格均上涨 0.1% 。三是消费品市场供给充⾜、需求平稳，价格总体稳定。消费品制造相关⾏业价
格总体保持稳定，其中纺织业、农副⻝品加⼯业价格分别上涨 0.2% 和 0.1% ；造纸和纸制品业、
酒饮料和精制茶制造业价格分别下降 0.4% 和 0.1% ；家具制造业、烟草制品业价格均持平。

## Document 566: 中国･５中全会で党内統治の引き締め強化も、景気には不透明感 ～習近平指導部の４期目入りを見据える一方、景気低迷と株安への対応が課題に～

Source PDF: all report pdf/中国･５中全会で党内統治の引き締め強化も、景気には不透明感 ～習近平指導部の４期目入りを見据える一方、景気低迷と株安への対応が課題に～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/4 
中国･５中全会で党内統治の引き締め強化も、景気には不透明感 
～習近平指導部の４期目入りを見据える一方、景気低迷と株安への対応が課題に～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 中国では、10 月 26～29 日に開催された中国共産党の５中全会において、党内統治の引き締
めや反腐敗の強化を柱とする決定が採択された。11 月１日に公表された決定の全文では、 党
への忠誠や幹部に対する監督の徹底が強調されるなど、2027 年の党大会での習近平指導部の
４期目入りを見据え、党組織に対する統制を一段と強化する狙いがうかがえる。 
○ 一方、中国経済を巡っては、当局が内需喚起に向けた財政・金融支援策を打ち出しているも
のの、10 月の製造業ＰＭＩは政府統計（49.3）と民間統計（52.0）で明暗が分かれた。非製
造業ＰＭＩ（49.7）も50 を下回るなど、企業マインドは依然として力強さを欠いている。 
○ さらに、原油高によるコスト上昇や不動産開発企業の経営環境悪化などが株式相場の重しと
なっている。こうしたなか、当局による市場支援への期待が高まっているものの、政策対応
が景気や金融市場の本格的な改善につながるかは不透明であり、先行きの動向には引き続き
注意が必要である。

【５中全会では、習近平指導部の４期目入りを見据え、党内統治の引き締め強化を図った】 
中国では、10 月 26 日から 29 日までの日程で中国共産党の第 20 期中央委員会第５回全体会議
（５中全会）が開催された（注１）。主要議題は「全面的かつ厳格な党内統治（全面従厳治党） 」と
され、2027 年に開催予定の中国共産党第21 回全国代表大会 （第21 回党大会）で習近平指導部の４
期目入りが視野に入るなか、その『前哨戦』として注目された。会議では、習氏が中央政治局を代
表して行った活動報告を聴取・討議し、 「全面的かつ厳格な党内統治を持続的に推進するうえでの
若干の重大問題に関する決定」 を採択した。同決定には、 党の規律、 幹部の選抜 ・ 監督、 組織運営、
反腐敗の強化といった内容が盛り込まれた。中央の指示を地方や各部門まで徹底し、 政治的な忠誠
と実務上の遂行能力を幹部管理に反映させるとともに、組織的な監督と責任追及の仕組みを整える
方針が示された。こうした決定の背景には、 「一時みられた党の指導力の弱体化・形骸化・希薄化と

注１ 10 月30 日付レポート「中共５中全会閉幕、次期党大会を見据えて党・軍への統制強化を加速」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/672289.html）

### Page 2

2/4 
いう厳しい現実を直視した」とする危機感があったとみられる。 
その後、11 月１日に公表された内決定の全文では、党への忠誠を重視する姿勢があらためて示さ
れるとともに、党内の『不純物やウイルス』を一掃すべく、反腐敗運動を引き続き強力に展開する
方針が示された。そのうえで、党員全体としては健全であると評価しつつも、思想面や組織面、仕
事ぶりにおける不純さの問題が根本的に解決されていないと指摘した。そして、 党への忠誠心を浸
透させる取り組みの強化、 日常業務や個人の言動、交友関係、 家族の価値観を通じて評価される 『党
性』 に基づく幹部登用、 『自己革命』を推進する必要性を強調した。具体的には、 幹部の個人資産な
どに関する申告内容の審査を厳格化するとともに、 家族の資産や支出が正当な所得に見合わない事
例の調査を含め、綿密かつ継続的な監督を行うとした。幹部の家族による事業活動についても、定
期的な監督の対象とすることを求めている。さらに、名義貸しによる株式保有や、暗号資産、ブロ
ックチェーン技術、 国境を越えた資金移動などを利用した汚職の取り締まりを強化する方針も示さ
れた。 
そのうえで、習氏を党の『核心』とする地位を断固として守るとともに、思想、政治、行動の各
方面で最高指導部と完全に足並みを揃えることを要求した。 習氏は、 決議について説明した演説で
も、「全面的かつ厳格な党内統治を粘り強く推進し、 党の健全性をむしばむ 『ウイルス』 を絶えず排
除することで、党の本質や理念を維持し、 『紅色の国（中国） 』を次世代に引き継ぐことができる」
との考えを示した。背景には、改革・解放後に生まれた若い世代の党幹部が増えるなか、思想面な
どで「新たな問題が生じている」との認識が広がっていることが影響している模様である。こうし
たことから、今回の５中全会には、習近平指導部が４期目入りを前に、党組織に対する統制を一段
と強化する狙いがあったと考えられる。

【当局による内需喚起の取り組みにもかかわらず、足元の企業マインドは依然として厳しい】 
一方で、足元の中国経済を巡っては、外需やハイテク関連を中心とする製造業をけん引役に景気
に持ち直しの動きがみられる一方、内需は力強さを欠く推移をみせている。こうしたなか、５中全
会では、マクロ政策の活用と内需拡大を求めるとともに、2026 年の経済・社会発展目標を達成する
方針が再確認された。中国ではここ数ヵ月、内需喚起に向けた取り組みを進める動きがみられる。
国務院は７月、同国で初めて個人消費に焦点を当てた「消費拡大の５ヵ年計画」を公表した。同計
画では、2030 年の小売売上高（社会消費品小売総額）の規模を2025 年（50.1 兆元）に比べて約２
割（60 兆元前後） 引き上げるべく、 消費構造の高度化や家計所得の引き上げを目指すとした。 加え
て、 共産党は７月末に開催した中央政治局会議において、 インフラ関連など財政支出の進捗加速に
よる景気下支えを図る方針を示した。これを受けて、政府・金融当局は８月以降、内需促進を目的
とする新たな財政・金融支援策を発表しており、今後はその効果の行方が注目される。

### Page 3

3/4 
ただし、足元においては、依然として企業を取り巻く環境は厳しい状況が続いている。国家統計
局が公表した10 月の製造業ＰＭＩ （購買
担当者景況感）は 49.3 となり、前月
（50.1）から▲0.8pt 低下して２ヵ月ぶ
りに好不況の分かれ目となる 50 を下回
る水準となった （図１） 。 足元の生産動向
を示す「生産（49.8） 」は前月比▲1.9pt
低下したほか、 「新規受注（49.4） 」も同
▲1.1pt、 「輸出向け新規受注 （48.7） 」 も
同▲1.3pt 低下していずれも50 を下回る
など、内・外需ともに弱含む動きが確認
された。受注動向の悪化による先行きの
減産懸念を反映して 「購買量 （48.9） 」 、 「輸入 （47.0） 」 ともに50 を下回る水準となるなど、原材料
や素材・部材に対する需要を抑制する動きもうかがえる。 生産調整の動きが強まったことを受けて
「雇用（48.4）」も引き続き50 を大きく下回るなど、家計部門を取り巻く環境は厳しく、家計消費
の足かせとなる展開が続いている。 
一方で、Ｓ＆Ｐグローバルが公表した10 月のRatingDog 製造業ＰＭＩは52.0 と前月（52.1）か
ら▲0.1pt 低下したものの、政府統計と
対照的に 50 を上回る推移が続いている
（図２）。足元の生産動向を示す「生産
（53.7） 」 は前月比＋0.1pt 上昇している
一方、 「新規受注（53.3） 」は同▲0.1pt、
「輸出向け新規受注（ 51.8） 」は同▲
2.0pt 低下しており、先行きの生産の行
方に不透明感が出ている。 受注動向に陰
りが出ているにもかかわらず「購買量
（53.0） 」は前月比＋0.5pt 上昇してお
り、悲観に傾いている兆しはみられな
い。 とはいえ、 生産拡大の動きが続いているものの 「雇用 （50.2） 」 は前月比▲0.3pt 低下しており、
製造業において幅広く雇用拡大の勢いは鈍化している。このように、 政府統計と民間統計では景況
感に違いがみられるものの、雇用を取り巻く環境は依然として力強さが欠けている。

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
国家統計局が公表した 10 月の非製造業ＰＭＩも 49.7 と前月（50.2）から▲0.5pt 低下して２ヵ
月ぶりに 50 を下回るなど、足元の企業
マインドの弱さを示唆している （図３） 。
業種別では、 前述の通りインフラ関連な
ど公共投資の進捗促進を目指す動きが
みられるにもかかわらず「建設業
（48.0） 」 は前月比▲2.3pt 低下して２ヵ
月ぶりに50 を下回るとともに、 「サービ
ス業 （50.0）」が同▲0.2pt 低下するなど
力強さを欠いている。 「新規受注 （44.4）」
も前月比▲ 2.1pt、 「輸出向け新規受注
（46.5） 」 も同▲2.0pt 低下してともに50
を大きく下回るなど、先行きにも不透明感が高まっている。 「雇用（46.0） 」は前月比＋0.1pt とわ
ずかに上昇しているものの、依然として50 を大きく下回るなど厳しい状況は変わっていない。

【株価低迷を受けて当局による市場支援観測が強まるも、不透明感の払しょくに繋がるか】 
イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油をはじめとする商品市況の高止まりを受けて、中国においても企
業部門はインフレに直面している。しかし、内需の弱さを理由に、企業部門においては原材料価格
の上昇を最終製品の価格に転嫁するハードルが高く、 採算が圧迫される展開が続いている。 そのう
え、当局が不動産開発に関連する規制強
化に動いたことを受けて、不動産開発企
業を取り巻く事業環境悪化 の懸念に加
え、債務問題の再燃も重なり、関連株を
中心に下押し圧力がかかったことも 株
式相場の重しとなっている（図４） 。 こう
したなか、中国人民銀行（中銀）と国家
金融監督管理総局、中国証券監督管理委
員会が２日、大手銀行や証券会社、資産
運用会社などと会合を開催し、このとこ
ろの株安の進行を受けて、資本市場を支
える手段に関する協議を行う旨の報道が出ている。政府の支援による買い支えの観測が出ている一
方、 政策措置が本格的な市場環境の改善を促すかは不透明なところも多く、 見通しが立ちにくい展
開が続く可能性には注意が必要である。

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
“蓝色狂潮”是民众对特朗普第二任期的强烈否认
民主党在美国中期选举中大获全胜，夺得两院控制权，在选民普遍不满经济状况和伊朗战争背景下，此结果可谓对特朗普第二任期的直接否定
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 在特朗普以压倒性优势返回白宫两年后，“蓝色狂潮”成为他的中期成绩单。民主党在美国中期选举中大获全胜，夺得国会两院控
制权。民主党在全美各地攻下共和党传统票仓，至少翻转7个本属于共和党参议院席位，并在多个共和党2024年轻松取胜的选区赢
得众议院选举。民主党的压倒性胜利意味着，自特朗普去年重返白宫以来，其在华盛顿的权力首次受到真正制衡。尤其需要注意
的是，一些特朗普明确背书的共和党参议员本次选举出现意外落败，凸显了特朗普本人或成为共和党政治上的拖累。

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
民主党在多个传统红州拿下州长席位
本次36个州换选州长，民主党在多个传统红州拿下州长，而州长对本州内AI数据中心建设等事务具有重要影响力，当前美国数据中心高度集中在近期摇摆州

资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究

◆ 州长对本州内诸如AI数据中心建设相关政策等事务具有重要影响力，本次中期选举，适逢AI主题权益板块承受逆风，民主党相较共和党尤
其是特朗普政府对AI产业在基建审批、行业监管等方面有着更为严苛政策立场，或倾向于延缓数据中心建设、拉长审批周期、提升开发和
投资成本等。

“Affordability Crisis”是特朗普的“政治成本”
美伊战争背景下的高油价和升息周期，带来的不仅仅是金融市场“参数变化”，更是加重了美国普通民众的“可负担性危机”
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 2025年，定义特朗普第二任期第一年的关键词
是混乱的关税战，但全球贸易的强大惯性、产
业链联通程度叠加横空出世的AI产业链强大的增
长引擎仍旧拖住全球经济韧性和资产的表现，
冲淡了特朗普的影响。
◆ 而2026年，持续性超出预期的美伊战争定义了
特朗普第二年，此时AI产业链增速边际放缓、资
本开支可持续性风险浮现，此时地缘-通胀风险
加大了全球经济和资产波动，从降息周期陡然
进入升息周期，带来的不仅仅是金融市场“参数
变化”，更是加重了美国普通民众的“可负担性危
机”。
◆ 在此背景下，中选过后是否有继续扩大美伊战
争的可能性？——地缘虽难以预测，但升级战

争恐继续消耗特朗普和GOP的政治筹码，甚至
继续拖累2028年大选。

### Page 6

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
美国政经变化几何？——三类路径看可为和不可为
参议院多数立法需60票（预算协调除外），且总统可否决，因此民主党掌控一院或两院，主要作用是阻止共和党议程，而非推行自己的议程
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 不依赖新立法、总统可自行决定：如232、301关税已在实际执行，对华301条款10月7日确认继续有效；先进芯片出口许可与对外
投资审查已有法律基础（《综合对外投资国家安全法》）、军事行动属总统权限——无太大变化，国会能做的只有拨款附加条件、
听证和诉讼，属于降低行政执行效率的施压手段。
◆ 需要新立法+参议院60票+总统签署：如企业税率从21%上调至25%—28%、恢复ACA增强补贴与联邦医保补贴、恢复被削减的IRA清
洁能源税惠、收回总统关税授权等——民主党推行自己的议程并非易事，对当前市场主线影响不大。普通法案在参议院需60票才
能结束辩论，推翻否决需两院各三分之二多数

◆ 必须通过的法案与参议院专属权力：如12月11日到期的临时拨款（覆盖全部12项年度拨款）、已在众议院以216:212通过的2027财
年国防授权法、延至12月11日的《国防生产法》授权、2027年债务上限；以及参议院人事确认权（美联储理事、联邦法官、内阁）
与众议院传票、监督权等。

### Page 7

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
共和党锚定败选因素直指战争，地缘再度升级民意压力较大
原油价格整体中枢居高不下，现货端有所回落供给紧缺有所缓和，但远月价
格逐步抬升，中长期溢价并未打破
原油结构看，当前并非定价现货紧缺关键，而是定价更持续的地缘溢价
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 但中期选举后，部分共和党明显将败选原因归因于“战争”，提升后续地缘升级的美国内部政治压力。
◆ 对于原油市场而言，我们的观点是“去繁存简”，中长期仍旧视作宏观风险。过去数周，原油市场核心叙事是“供给已然回归，较战
前缺口减少”，但若从更大的角度上看，去繁存简，当地缘核心矛盾未解决的情况下：1）供给持续回归具有脆弱性（伊朗仍有袭
击海峡邮轮的能力、胡塞武装仍有干扰沙特原油供应能力。2）运费价格居高不下、全球原油库存较低支持原油估值。这使得当前
静态供给回归无法有效体现在油价回落、宏观风险缓和这一逻辑上。

联储加息周期定价尚未结束，但空间亦有限
FOMC按兵不动，前方整体定价本轮加息周期不足两次，较9月底鹰派定价峰
